#[test]
fn media_link_revocation_and_expiry_have_separate_custody() {
    let mut e = engine("table mediaDocuments:");
    let d = write(
        &mut e,
        "mediaDocument",
        "alice",
        json!({"title":"private image"}),
        1000,
    );
    let asset = e.store_asset("alice", PNG, 1000).unwrap();
    write(
        &mut e,
        "placeMedia",
        "alice",
        json!({"document":d["id"],"image":wire::to_json(&asset.column_value())}),
        1000,
    );
    assert!(e.asset_access("bob", &asset.id, 1000).is_err());
    let secret = "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef";
    write(
        &mut e,
        "mediaLink",
        "service:ops",
        json!({"document":d["id"],"secret":secret,"expiresAt":2000}),
        1000,
    );
    write(&mut e, "claimMedia", "bob", json!({"secret":secret}), 1000);
    let args = json!({"document":d["id"]});
    assert!(!read(&mut e, "mediaDocumentById", "bob", args.clone(), 1000).is_null());
    assert!(e.asset_access("bob", &asset.id, 1000).is_ok());
    write(
        &mut e,
        "revokeMediaLink",
        "service:ops",
        json!({"secret":secret}),
        1000,
    );
    fail(
        &mut e,
        "claimMedia",
        "carol",
        json!({"secret":secret}),
        1000,
        "BAD_LINK",
    );
    assert!(!read(&mut e, "mediaDocumentById", "bob", args.clone(), 1000).is_null());
    write(
        &mut e,
        "revokeMediaReader",
        "service:ops",
        json!({"document":d["id"],"who":"bob"}),
        1000,
    );
    assert!(read(&mut e, "mediaDocumentById", "bob", args.clone(), 1000).is_null());
    assert!(e.asset_access("bob", &asset.id, 1000).is_err());
    let secret2 = "fedcba9876543210fedcba9876543210";
    write(
        &mut e,
        "mediaLink",
        "service:ops",
        json!({"document":d["id"],"secret":secret2,"expiresAt":2000}),
        1000,
    );
    write(
        &mut e,
        "claimMedia",
        "carol",
        json!({"secret":secret2}),
        1000,
    );
    assert!(!read(&mut e, "mediaDocumentById", "carol", args.clone(), 1999).is_null());
    assert!(read(&mut e, "mediaDocumentById", "carol", args.clone(), 2000).is_null());
    assert!(e.asset_access("carol", &asset.id, 2000).is_err());
    assert!(!read(&mut e, "mediaDocumentById", "alice", args, 2000).is_null());
}

#[test]
fn public_metadata_self_join_does_not_publish_private_payload() {
    let mut e = engine("table clubCatalogue:");
    for open in [false, true] {
        let c = write(
            &mut e,
            "catalogueClub",
            "alice",
            json!({"title":"visible","open":open,"body":"private"}),
            1000,
        );
        let args = json!({"club":c["id"]});
        assert_eq!(
            read(&mut e, "catalogueClubById", "bob", args.clone(), 1000)["title"],
            "visible"
        );
        assert!(read(&mut e, "clubSheet", "bob", args.clone(), 1000).is_null());
        if open {
            write(&mut e, "selfJoinClub", "bob", args.clone(), 1000);
            assert_eq!(
                read(&mut e, "clubSheet", "bob", args, 1000)["body"],
                "private"
            );
        } else {
            fail(&mut e, "selfJoinClub", "bob", args, 1000, "E_RULE");
        }
    }
}
#[test]
fn hidden_only_page_is_complete_empty_and_never_a_denial() {
    let source = "table readers:\n  scope: text <=20\n  principal: principal\n  unique scope, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable secrets:\n  scope: text <=20\n  body: text <=80\n  read <- exists readers[.scope, viewer]\n  online only\nquery page(c: cursor ?):\n  return secrets where .scope = 'private' first 20 by .id after c\n";
    let b = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
    seed(
        &mut e,
        "secrets",
        json!({"id":"s","scope":"private","body":"hidden"}),
    );
    let c = ctx(&e, "page", "bob", json!({"c":null}), 1000);
    let page = e.query("page", c).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(wire::to_json(&page.data), json!([]));
    assert!(page.complete);
    assert!(page.next.is_none());
    assert!(page.cut.is_none());
}

A rule cannot scan "all participants consented". Store one grant after a mutation checks uniquely addressed consent rows, and delete it when consent is withdrawn. This two-person recipe makes those exact probes readable to the organizer; it deliberately reveals consent to that organizer. For an arbitrary population use an authorized maintained count and compare it with the expected population (`guide recipes`, booking); never scan partly readable consent rows.

```quarry
table agreements:
  owner: principal
  first: principal
  second: principal
  immutable owner, first, second
  read <- .owner = viewer or .first = viewer or .second = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  online only
table consents:
  agreement: agreements
  principal: principal
  unique agreement, principal
  immutable agreement, principal
  read <- .principal = viewer or exists agreements[.agreement, owner = viewer]
  insert <- .principal = viewer and (exists agreements[.agreement, first = viewer] or exists agreements[.agreement, second = viewer])
  delete <- .principal = viewer
  online only
table access:
  agreement: agreements
  principal: principal
  unique agreement, principal
  read <- .principal = viewer or exists agreements[.agreement, first = viewer] or exists agreements[.agreement, second = viewer]
  insert <- exists agreements[.agreement, owner = viewer]
  delete <- exists agreements[.agreement, first = viewer] or exists agreements[.agreement, second = viewer]
  online only
mutation consent(agreement: agreements):
  return insert consents { agreement, principal: viewer }
mutation open(agreement: agreements):
  a = agreements[agreement] ! else NOT_FOUND
  require a.owner = viewer else NOT_OWNER
  require exists consents[agreement, a.first] and exists consents[agreement, a.second] else CONSENT_NEEDED
  return insert access { agreement, principal: a.owner }
mutation withdraw(agreement: agreements):
  delete consents[agreement, viewer]
  a = agreements[agreement] ! else NOT_FOUND
  g = access[agreement, a.owner]
  if g != null:
    delete access[g.id]
query granted(agreement: agreements):
  return exists access[agreement, viewer]
```

Participants can read and delete the grant, so withdrawal atomically removes it. Every consent or participant change must maintain this grant in the same transaction; avoid separate grant repair jobs that leave a disclosure window.

## Copy on reveal: an event opens the payload

Keep the secret in a private table. An authorized event mutation copies it into an independently readable table in the same commit. Returning a shape that omits a secret is not a column read rule. The private lookup already requires the owner; an outsider gets NOT_FOUND. Reveal is durable; undoing the event flag does not retract an already copied payload. To retract it, delete the copy.

```quarry
table drafts:
  owner: principal
  body: text 1..200
  immutable owner
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  online only
table revealed:
  draft: drafts
  body: text 1..200
  unique draft
  public 'the author explicitly published this copy'
  insert <- exists drafts[.draft, owner = viewer]
  online only
mutation draft(body: text 1..200):
  return insert drafts { owner: viewer, body }
mutation reveal(draft: drafts):
  d = drafts[draft] ! else NOT_FOUND
  return insert revealed { draft, body: d.body }
query publication(publication: revealed):
  return revealed[publication]
```

For time instead of an event, use `not before .opensAt` and `expire at .endsAt` (the timed-disclosure recipe above); clocks stay out of read rules.

## Share links: token enrollment, then principal reads

The supported link grants an authenticated visitor a principal row. Possession of a high-entropy token authorizes enrollment; the resulting read rule uses the principal. Token rows are program-readable, but expose no listing operation or token row result. Generate secrets outside Quarry and provision them with a service seat. Hashing, leakage prevention and delivery of the link belong to the app. Revoking the token prevents new enrollment; revoke grant rows separately to remove existing readers. A query argument alone is not a capability grant.

```quarry
table documents:
  owner: principal
  body: text 1..200
  immutable owner
  read <- .owner = viewer or exists readers[.id, viewer] or viewer = 'service:ops'
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  online only
table tokens:
  secret: text 32..100
  document: documents
  unique secret
  immutable secret, document
  read <- allow
  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops'
  delete <- viewer = 'service:ops'
  online only
table readers:
  document: documents
  principal: principal
  unique document, principal
  immutable document, principal
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
  insert <- .principal = viewer
  delete <- viewer = 'service:ops'
  online only
mutation provisionLink(document: documents, secret: text 32..100):
  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR
  d = documents[document] ! else NOT_FOUND
  return insert tokens { document: d.id, secret }
mutation revokeLink(secret: text 32..100):
  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR
  t = tokens[secret = secret] ! else BAD_LINK
  delete tokens[t.id]
mutation revokeReader(document: documents, principal: principal):
  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR
  delete readers[document, principal]
mutation claim(secret: text 32..100):
  t = tokens[secret = secret] ! else BAD_LINK
  return insert readers { document: t.document, principal: viewer }
query document(document: documents):
  return documents[document]
```

Do not publish a generic mutation accepting a document id and inserting a reader: that would bypass token enrollment. Mutations are the app's API; `insert <- .principal = viewer` alone does not prove token possession.

## Split-table anonymity

Private author attribution belongs in its own table; the published payload has no author field. Public replies and private audit rows are linked by id, so operators may still correlate them. This is application anonymity from other readers, not a claim about network logs or the store administrator.

```quarry
table responses:
  body: text 1..200
  public 'anonymous response bodies'
  insert <- allow
  online only
table attribution:
  response: responses
  principal: principal
  unique response
  immutable response, principal
  read <- .principal = viewer
  insert <- .principal = viewer and created responses[.response]
  online only
mutation respond(body: text 1..200):
  r = insert responses { body }
  insert attribution { response: r.id, principal: viewer }
  return r
query author(response: responses):
//! Published authorization examples execute under their authored rules.
use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value, WriteState};
use snapback4_lang::guide;
const RECIPES: &str = include_str!("../src/guide/rule_recipes.md");
fn engine(marker: &str) -> Engine {
    let source = guide::quarry(RECIPES)
        .into_iter()
        .find(|s| s.contains(marker))
        .unwrap();
    let b = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{marker}: {:?}", b.diagnostics);
    assert!(b.notes.is_empty(), "{marker}: {:?}", b.notes);
    Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap()
}
fn id(s: &str) -> Value {
    Value::Id(s.into())
}
fn text(s: &str) -> Value {
    Value::String(s.into())
}
fn ctx(w: &str, p: &[(&str, Value)]) -> Context {
    Context::new(
        w,
        p.iter().map(|(k, v)| (k.to_string(), v.clone())).collect(),
    )
    .at(1000)
}
fn row(t: &str, p: &[(&str, Value)]) -> (String, Value) {
    (
        t.into(),
        snapback4_core::value::object(p.iter().map(|(k, v)| (*k, v.clone()))),
    )
}
fn write(e: &mut Engine, op: &str, w: &str, p: &[(&str, Value)]) -> Value {
    match e.mutate(op, None, ctx(w, p)).unwrap().state {
        WriteState::Sent { result, .. } => result,
        WriteState::Failed { why, .. } => panic!("{op}: {} {}", why.code, why.message),
    }
}
fn read(e: &mut Engine, op: &str, w: &str, p: &[(&str, Value)]) -> Value {
    e.query(op, ctx(w, p)).unwrap().data
}
fn refused(e: &mut Engine, op: &str, w: &str, p: &[(&str, Value)]) {
    assert!(matches!(
        e.mutate(op, None, ctx(w, p)).unwrap().state,
        WriteState::Failed { .. }
    ));
}
#[test]
fn clean_recipes() {
    for m in [
        "table folders:",
        "table agreements:",
        "table drafts:",
        "table tokens:",
        "table responses:",
        "table bands:",
        "table library:",
    ] {
        engine(m);
    }
}
#[test]
fn copied_audience() {
    let mut e = engine("table folders:");
    let f = write(&mut e, "addFolder", "alice", &[])
        .field("id")
        .unwrap()
        .clone();
    let r = write(
        &mut e,
        "addFile",
        "alice",
        &[("folder", f.clone()), ("title", text("private"))],
    )
    .field("id")
    .unwrap()
    .clone();
    assert_eq!(
        read(&mut e, "file", "bob", &[("file", r.clone())]),
        Value::Null
    );
    assert_eq!(
        read(&mut e, "file", "alice", &[("file", r)]).field("title"),
        Ok(&text("private"))
    );
    refused(
        &mut e,
        "addFile",
        "bob",
        &[("folder", f), ("title", text("wrong"))],
    );
}
#[test]
fn consent_revocation() {
    let mut e = engine("table agreements:");
    e.seed([row(
        "agreements",
        &[
            ("id", id("a")),
            ("owner", id("owner")),
            ("first", id("alice")),
            ("second", id("bob")),
        ],
    )])
    .unwrap();
    let p = [("agreement", id("a"))];
    write(&mut e, "consent", "alice", &p);
    refused(&mut e, "open", "owner", &p);
    write(&mut e, "consent", "bob", &p);
    write(&mut e, "open", "owner", &p);
    assert_eq!(read(&mut e, "granted", "owner", &p), Value::Bool(true));
    assert_eq!(read(&mut e, "granted", "outsider", &p), Value::Bool(false));
    write(&mut e, "withdraw", "alice", &p);
    assert_eq!(read(&mut e, "granted", "owner", &p), Value::Bool(false));
}
#[test]
fn event_reveal() {
    let mut e = engine("table drafts:");
    let d = write(&mut e, "draft", "alice", &[("body", text("secret"))])
        .field("id")
        .unwrap()
        .clone();
    refused(&mut e, "reveal", "bob", &[("draft", d.clone())]);
    let r = write(&mut e, "reveal", "alice", &[("draft", d)])
        .field("id")
        .unwrap()
        .clone();
    assert_eq!(
        read(&mut e, "publication", "bob", &[("publication", r)]).field("body"),
        Ok(&text("secret"))
    );
}
#[test]
fn token_enrollment() {
    let mut e = engine("table tokens:");
    let secret = "012345678901234567890123456789012345";
    e.seed([
        row(
            "documents",
            &[
                ("id", id("d")),
                ("owner", id("alice")),
                ("body", text("linked")),
            ],
        ),
        row(
            "tokens",
            &[
                ("id", id("t")),
                ("document", id("d")),
                ("secret", text(secret)),
            ],
        ),
    ])
    .unwrap();
    let p = [("document", id("d"))];
    assert_eq!(read(&mut e, "document", "bob", &p), Value::Null);
    refused(
        &mut e,
        "claim",
        "bob",
        &[("secret", text("00000000000000000000000000000000"))],
    );
    write(&mut e, "claim", "bob", &[("secret", text(secret))]);
    assert_eq!(
        read(&mut e, "document", "bob", &p).field("body"),
        Ok(&text("linked"))
    );
    assert_eq!(read(&mut e, "document", "carol", &p), Value::Null);
    write(
        &mut e,
        "revokeLink",
        "service:ops",
        &[("secret", text(secret))],
    );
    refused(&mut e, "claim", "carol", &[("secret", text(secret))]);
    assert_eq!(
        read(&mut e, "document", "bob", &p).field("body"),
        Ok(&text("linked"))
    );
    write(
        &mut e,
        "revokeReader",
        "service:ops",
        &[("document", id("d")), ("principal", id("bob"))],
    );
    assert_eq!(read(&mut e, "document", "bob", &p), Value::Null);
}
#[test]
fn split_anonymity() {
    let mut e = engine("table responses:");
    let r = write(&mut e, "respond", "alice", &[("body", text("anonymous"))]);
    assert!(r.field("principal").is_err());
    let p = [("response", r.field("id").unwrap().clone())];
    assert_eq!(read(&mut e, "author", "bob", &p), Value::Null);
    assert_eq!(
        read(&mut e, "author", "alice", &p).field("principal"),
        Ok(&id("alice"))
    );
    assert_eq!(
        read(&mut e, "response", "bob", &p).field("body"),
        Ok(&text("anonymous"))
    );
}
#[test]
fn version_pointer() {
    let mut e = engine("table bands:");
    e.seed([
        row("bands", &[("id", id("b")), ("owner", id("alice"))]),
        row(
            "fans",
            &[("id", id("f")), ("band", id("b")), ("principal", id("bob"))],
        ),
        row(
            "versions",
            &[
                ("id", id("v1")),
                ("band", id("b")),
                ("owner", id("alice")),
                ("title", text("first")),
                ("cover", Value::Null),
            ],
        ),
        row(
            "versions",
            &[
                ("id", id("v2")),
                ("band", id("b")),
                ("owner", id("alice")),
                ("title", text("second")),
                ("cover", Value::Null),
            ],
        ),
    ])
    .unwrap();
    let p = [("version", id("v1"))];
    assert_eq!(read(&mut e, "version", "bob", &p), Value::Null);
    write(
        &mut e,
        "publish",
        "alice",
        &[("band", id("b")), ("version", id("v1"))],
    );
    assert_eq!(
        read(&mut e, "version", "bob", &p).field("title"),
        Ok(&text("first"))
    );
    write(
        &mut e,
        "publish",
        "alice",
        &[("band", id("b")), ("version", id("v2"))],
    );
    assert_eq!(read(&mut e, "version", "bob", &p), Value::Null);
    assert_eq!(
        read(&mut e, "version", "carol", &[("version", id("v2"))]),
        Value::Null
    );
}
#[test]
fn visitors_and_teams() {
    let mut e = engine("table library:");
    e.seed([
        row("teams", &[("id", id("t")), ("owner", id("alice"))]),
        row(
            "members",
            &[("id", id("m")), ("team", id("t")), ("principal", id("bob"))],
        ),
        row(
            "library",
            &[
                ("id", id("public")),
                ("team", id("t")),
                ("owner", id("alice")),
                ("shared", Value::Bool(true)),
                ("title", text("public")),
            ],
        ),
        row(
            "library",
            &[
                ("id", id("private")),
                ("team", id("t")),
                ("owner", id("alice")),
                ("shared", Value::Bool(false)),
                ("title", text("private")),
            ],
        ),
    ])
    .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        read(
            &mut e,
            "entry",
            "guest:visitor",
            &[("entry", id("private"))]
        ),
        Value::Null
    );
    assert_eq!(
        read(&mut e, "entry", "guest:visitor", &[("entry", id("public"))]).field("title"),
        Ok(&text("public"))
    );
    assert_eq!(
        read(&mut e, "entry", "bob", &[("entry", id("private"))]).field("title"),
        Ok(&text("private"))
    );
    for (op, who, p, count) in [
        ("mine", "alice", vec![("c", Value::Null)], 2),
        (
            "publicLibrary",
            "guest:visitor",
            vec![("c", Value::Null)],
            1,
        ),
        (
            "teamLibrary",
            "bob",
            vec![("team", id("t")), ("c", Value::Null)],
            2,
        ),
    ] {
        let page = e.query(op, ctx(who, &p)).unwrap();
        assert!(page.complete, "{op}: all intended rows are known");
        assert_eq!(page.data.as_array().unwrap().len(), count);
    }
}

const PRODUCTION: &str = include_str!("../src/guide/production.md");
#[test]
fn production_accounts_enroll_privately_and_survive_rollback() {
    let source = guide::quarry(PRODUCTION).remove(0);
    let compile = |source: String| {
        let b = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
        assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
        assert!(b.notes.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.notes);
        b
    };
    let first = compile(source.clone());
    let mut e =
        Engine::memory(first.schema, first.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
    let mut entropy = 1234;
    let alice = snapback4_core::identity::signup(
        e.store(),
        "alice@example.test",
        "private-password",
        1000,
        &mut entropy,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let bob = snapback4_core::identity::signup(
        e.store(),
        "bob@example.test",
        "private-password",
        1000,
        &mut entropy,
    )
    .unwrap();
    write(
        &mut e,
        "register",
        &alice.principal,
        &[("displayName", text("Alice"))],
    );
    write(
        &mut e,
        "register",
        &bob.principal,
        &[("displayName", text("Bob"))],
    );
    refused(
        &mut e,
        "grantStaff",
        &bob.principal,
        &[("principal", id(&bob.principal))],
    );
    write(
        &mut e,
        "grantStaff",
        "service:ops",
        &[("principal", id(&alice.principal))],
    );
    refused(
        &mut e,
        "provisionCatalogue",
        &bob.principal,
        &[("slug", text("standard")), ("label", text("Unauthorized"))],
    );
    write(
        &mut e,
        "provisionCatalogue",
        "service:ops",
        &[("slug", text("standard")), ("label", text("Standard"))],
    );
    assert_eq!(e.store().rows("catalogues").unwrap().len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(
        read(&mut e, "catalogue", &bob.principal, &[("c", Value::Null)])
            .as_array()
            .unwrap()[0]
            .field("label"),
        Ok(&text("Standard"))
    );
    let team = write(
        &mut e,
        "createTeam",
        &alice.principal,
        &[("name", text("Production"))],
    )
    .field("id")
    .unwrap()
    .clone();
    write(
        &mut e,
        "enroll",
        &alice.principal,
        &[("team", team.clone()), ("principal", id(&bob.principal))],
    );
    refused(
        &mut e,
        "enroll",
        &bob.principal,
        &[("team", team.clone()), ("principal", id(&bob.principal))],
    );
    let directory = e.query("directory", ctx(&bob.principal, &[("c", Value::Null)]));
    assert_eq!(directory.unwrap_err().code, "NOT_STAFF");
    let directory = read(&mut e, "directory", &alice.principal, &[("c", Value::Null)]);
    assert_eq!(directory.as_array().unwrap().len(), 2);
    assert_eq!(
        read(
            &mut e,
            "teammates",
            &bob.principal,
            &[("team", team), ("c", Value::Null)]
        )
        .as_array()
        .unwrap()
        .len(),
        1
    );
    let upgraded = source.replace(
        "  displayName: text 1..80",
        "  displayName: text 1..80\n  bio: text <=80 ?",
    ) + "\nmutation setBio(bio: text <=80):\n  return update profiles[principal = viewer] { bio }\n";
    let v2 = compile(upgraded.clone());
    e.adopt_release(
        v2.schema,
        v2.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program),
        &snapback4_core::engine::release::ReleaseActivation::new(2, None),
        false,
    )
    .unwrap();
    write(
        &mut e,
        "setBio",
        &alice.principal,
        &[("bio", text("Restored on forward"))],
    );
    // Exercise a real account session against the adopted generation.
    let result = e
        .mutate(
            "register",
            None,
            ctx(&alice.principal, &[("displayName", text("Alice Updated"))])
                .with_session(&alice.token),
        )
        .unwrap();
    assert!(matches!(result.state, WriteState::Sent { .. }));
    let v1 = compile(source);
    e.adopt_release(
        v1.schema,
        v1.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program),
        &snapback4_core::engine::release::ReleaseActivation::new(1, Some(2)),
        false,
    )
    .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        read(&mut e, "myProfile", &alice.principal, &[]).field("displayName"),
        Ok(&text("Alice Updated"))
    );
    let login =
        snapback4_core::identity::login(e.store(), "alice@example.test", "private-password", 1001)
            .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(login.principal, alice.principal);
    let v2 = compile(upgraded);
    e.adopt_release(
        v2.schema,
        v2.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program),
        &snapback4_core::engine::release::ReleaseActivation::new(2, Some(1)),
        false,
    )
    .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        read(&mut e, "myProfile", &alice.principal, &[]).field("displayName"),
        Ok(&text("Alice Updated"))
    );
    assert_eq!(
        read(&mut e, "myProfile", &alice.principal, &[]).field("bio"),
        Ok(&text("Restored on forward"))
    );
}

#[test]
fn service_seat_program_probes_need_the_explicit_read_branch() {
    let source = guide::quarry(guide::card("deploy").unwrap()).remove(0)
        + "\nquery probe(principal: principal):\n  return exists admins[principal = principal]\n";
    for (schema, expected) in [
        (source.clone(), true),
        (
            source.replace(
                "read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'",
                "read <- .principal = viewer",
            ),
            false,
        ),
    ] {
        let b = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), schema)]);
        assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
        let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
        write(
            &mut e,
            "grantAdmin",
            "service:ops",
            &[("principal", id("user:alice"))],
        );
        assert_eq!(
            read(
                &mut e,
                "probe",
                "service:ops",
                &[("principal", id("user:alice"))]
            ),
            Value::Bool(expected)
        );
    }
}

#[test]
fn twice_written_row_returns_the_bound_snapshot_not_the_last_image() {
    let source = "table records:\n  body: text <=80\n  public 'snapshot test'\n  insert <- allow\n  update <- allow\n  online only\nmutation original():\n  r = insert records { body: 'before' }\n  update records[r.id] { body: 'after' }\n  return r\nquery record(record: records):\n  return records[record]\n";
    let b = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    assert!(b.notes.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.notes);
    let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
    let returned = write(&mut e, "original", "alice", &[]);
    assert_eq!(returned.field("body"), Ok(&text("before")));
    let stored = read(
        &mut e,
        "record",
        "alice",
        &[("record", returned.field("id").unwrap().clone())],
    );
    assert_eq!(stored.field("body"), Ok(&text("after")));
}

/// `{ column: value }` a read pins, its keys resolved against the table's
/// indexes (named keys, or positional keys of the one unique index of that
/// width) and its `where` equalities. None when a positional key is ambiguous.
pub(super) fn pins(catalog: &Catalog, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<BTreeMap<String, Expr>> {
    let info = catalog.tables.get(&read.table)?;
    let mut out = BTreeMap::new();
    let positional: Vec<&KeyPart> = read.keys.iter().filter(|k| k.column.is_none()).collect();
    if !positional.is_empty() {
        let columns = match read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|index| &index.name == name)) {
            Some(index) => index.columns.clone(),
            None => {
                let unique: Vec<&IndexDecl> = info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns.len() == read.keys.len() && !index.columns.iter().any(|c| c == "id")).collect();
                match unique.as_slice() { [index] => index.columns.clone(), _ => return None }
            }
        };
        if columns.len() < read.keys.len() { return None; }
        for (column, key) in columns.iter().zip(&read.keys) { out.insert(key.column.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| column.clone()), key.value.clone()); }
    } else {
        for key in &read.keys { out.insert(key.column.clone()?, key.value.clone()); }
    }
    let mut filters = Vec::new();
    if let Some(filter) = &read.filter { super::intent::split((**filter).clone(), &mut filters); }
    for filter in filters {
        if let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = &filter && op == "="
            && let Some(column) = super::intent::column(left) { out.entry(column.to_owned()).or_insert_with(|| (**right).clone()); }
    }
    Some(out)
}

/// Every `exists` a condition asserts (outside `not`): `a and exists t[…]`, either side of `or`.
fn asserted_probes<'a>(condition: &'a Expr, out: &mut Vec<&'a ReadExpr>) {
    match condition {
        Expr::Exists { read, .. } => out.push(read),
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" || op == "or" => { asserted_probes(left, out); asserted_probes(right, out); }
        _ => {}
    }
}

fn requires(body: &[Stmt], out: &mut Vec<(Expr, String)>) {
    for statement in body {
        match statement {
            Stmt::Require { condition, code, .. } => out.push((condition.clone(), code.clone())),
            Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => { requires(then_body, out); requires(else_body, out); }
            Stmt::For { body, .. } | Stmt::As { body, .. } => requires(body, out),
            _ => {}
        }
    }
}

fn hidden_requires(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, programs: &[&ProgramDecl], notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
    let mut seen = BTreeSet::new();
    for program in programs {
        let mut tested = Vec::new();
        requires(&program.prelude, &mut tested);
        requires(&program.body, &mut tested);
        for (condition, code) in &tested {
            let mut probes = Vec::new();
            asserted_probes(condition, &mut probes);
            for read in probes {
                let Some(table) = schema.table(&read.table) else { continue };
                let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { continue };
                if table.through.is_some() || info.maintained.is_some() || table.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable() { continue; }
                let Some(pinned) = pins(catalog, read) else { continue };
                // Every read disjunct admits only the row whose pinned `column` is the caller,
                // and the probe pins that column to something else.
                let owners: Vec<Option<(String, String)>> = table.rules.read.disjuncts().into_iter().map(|disjunct| conjuncts(disjunct).into_iter().find_map(|c| match c {
                    RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(column), RuleTerm::Viewer) | RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Field(column)) =>
                        pinned.get(column).filter(|value| !is_viewer(value)).map(|value| (column.clone(), crate::print::expression(value))),
                    _ => None,
                })).collect();
                if owners.is_empty() || owners.iter().any(Option::is_none) { continue; }
                let Some((column, value)) = owners[0].clone() else { continue };
                if !seen.insert((program.name.clone(), read.site.to_string())) { continue; }
                let probe = crate::print::expression(&Expr::Exists { read: read.clone(), site: read.site.clone() });
                let site = rule_site(catalog, &read.table, Effect::Read).map(|s| format!(" ({s})")).unwrap_or_default();
                notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN",
                    format!("{} {}: `{probe}` reads {} under its read rule `{}`{site}, which admits only the row whose {column} is the caller, so this require passes only when `{value}` is the caller: for every other {} row, which exists but is hidden, it refuses {code} (a program `exists` sees only readable rows; a rule probe sees every row)",
                        if program.kind == ProgramKind::Query { "query" } else { "mutation" }, program.name, read.table, table.rules.read.quarry(), read.table),
                    format!("let the callers who run this check read those rows: `read <- {} or exists {}[viewer]` (a roster its members read), or a role (`… or exists staff[viewer]`); if only the caller's own row is meant, write `{column} = viewer`", table.rules.read.quarry(), read.table)).guide("rules"));
            }
//! Mutable syntax traversal shared by planning and final index-name resolution.
use crate::ast::*;

pub(crate) fn expression(value: &mut Expr, visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    match value {
        Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Exists { read, .. } | Expr::Created { read, .. } => read_children(read, visit),
        Expr::Unary { value, .. } | Expr::Member { base: value, .. } | Expr::Assert { value, .. } | Expr::ShapeUse { base: value, .. } => expression(value, visit),
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => { expression(left, visit); expression(right, visit); }
        Expr::Array { items, .. } | Expr::Call { args: items, .. } => { for item in items { expression(item, visit); } }
        Expr::Object { fields, .. } => { for field in fields { if let Some(value) = &mut field.value { expression(value, visit); } } }
        Expr::Shape { base, fields, .. } => { expression(base, visit); shape(fields, visit); }
        Expr::If { then, condition, otherwise, .. } => { expression(then, visit); expression(condition, visit); expression(otherwise, visit); }
        Expr::Walk { seed, .. } => expression(seed, visit),
        Expr::Write { keys, fields, condition, .. } => {
            for key in keys { expression(&mut key.value, visit); }
            for field in fields { if let Some(value) = &mut field.value { expression(value, visit); } }
            if let Some(value) = condition { expression(value, visit); }
        }
        Expr::Merge(merge) => {
            for value in [&mut merge.matching, &mut merge.filter, &mut merge.cursor].into_iter().flatten() { expression(value, visit); }
            let mut lanes = Vec::new();
            for mut lane in std::mem::take(&mut merge.lanes) {
                if let Some(predicate) = &mut lane.admission { expression(predicate, visit); }
                if let Some((_, read)) = &mut lane.nulls {
                    let mut value = Expr::Read(*read.clone());
                    expression(&mut value, visit);
                    let Expr::Read(planned) = value else { unreachable!("planned null scan") };
                    **read = planned;
                }
                if let Some(value) = &mut lane.distinct { expression(value, visit); }
                if let Some(fields) = &mut lane.project { shape(fields, visit); }
                for value in &mut lane.prefix { expression(value, visit); }
                if let Some((_, value)) = &mut lane.loop_ { expression(value, visit); }
                if let Some(read) = &mut lane.planned {
                    let mut value = Expr::Read(*read.clone());
                    expression(&mut value, visit);
                    match value {
                        Expr::Read(planned) => **read = planned,
                        Expr::Merge(nested) if lane.loop_.is_none() => {
                            for mut child in nested.lanes {
                                child.branch = lane.branch.clone();
                                child.project = lane.project.clone();
                                lanes.push(child);
                            }
                            continue;
                        }
                        _ => unreachable!("a planned lane is a read or an expanded read"),
                    }
                }
                lanes.push(lane);
            }
            merge.lanes = lanes;
        }
        Expr::Name { .. } | Expr::Focal { .. } | Expr::Literal { .. } => {}
    }
    visit(value);
}

fn read_children(read: &mut ReadExpr, visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    for key in &mut read.keys { expression(&mut key.value, visit); }
    for seek in &mut read.seeks { expression(&mut seek.value, visit); }
    for value in [&mut read.filter, &mut read.matching].into_iter().flatten() { expression(value, visit); }
    if let Some(value) = read.range.as_mut().and_then(|range| range.cursor.as_mut()) { expression(value, visit); }
}

pub(super) fn shape(fields: &mut [ShapeField], visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    for field in fields { if let ShapeField::Field { value, .. } = field { expression(value, visit); } }
}

pub(super) fn statements(body: &mut [Stmt], visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    for stmt in body {
        match stmt {
            Stmt::Bind { value, .. } | Stmt::Expr { value, .. } | Stmt::Return { value, .. } => expression(value, visit),
            Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => expression(condition, visit),
            Stmt::Emit { payload, once, .. } => {
                expression(payload, visit);
                if let Some(key) = once { expression(key, visit); }
            }
            Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body, .. } => { expression(condition, visit); statements(then_body, visit); statements(else_body, visit); }
            Stmt::As { principal: domain, body, .. } | Stmt::For { domain, body, .. } => { expression(domain, visit); statements(body, visit); }
            Stmt::Break { .. } | Stmt::Continue { .. } => {}
        }
    }
}

pub(crate) fn document(doc: &mut Document, visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    for program in &mut doc.programs { statements(&mut program.prelude, visit); statements(&mut program.body, visit); }
    for function in &mut doc.functions {
        match &mut function.body { FunctionBody::Expression(value) => expression(value, visit), FunctionBody::Block(body) => statements(body, visit) }
    }
    for decl in &mut doc.shapes { shape(&mut decl.fields, visit); }
}

#[test]
fn media_link_revocation_and_expiry_have_separate_custody() {
    let mut e = engine("table mediaDocuments:");
    let d = write(
        &mut e,
        "mediaDocument",
        "alice",
        json!({"title":"private image"}),
        1000,
    );
    let asset = e.store_asset("alice", PNG, 1000).unwrap();
    write(
        &mut e,
        "placeMedia",
        "alice",
        json!({"document":d["id"],"image":wire::to_json(&asset.column_value())}),
        1000,
    );
    assert!(e.asset_access("bob", &asset.id, 1000).is_err());
    let secret = "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef";
    write(
        &mut e,
        "mediaLink",
        "service:ops",
        json!({"document":d["id"],"secret":secret,"expiresAt":2000}),
        1000,
    );
    write(&mut e, "claimMedia", "bob", json!({"secret":secret}), 1000);
    let args = json!({"document":d["id"]});
    assert!(!read(&mut e, "mediaDocumentById", "bob", args.clone(), 1000).is_null());
    assert!(e.asset_access("bob", &asset.id, 1000).is_ok());
    write(
        &mut e,
        "revokeMediaLink",
        "service:ops",
        json!({"secret":secret}),
        1000,
    );
    fail(
        &mut e,
        "claimMedia",
        "carol",
        json!({"secret":secret}),
        1000,
        "BAD_LINK",
    );
    assert!(!read(&mut e, "mediaDocumentById", "bob", args.clone(), 1000).is_null());
    write(
        &mut e,
        "revokeMediaReader",
        "service:ops",
        json!({"document":d["id"],"who":"bob"}),
        1000,
    );
    assert!(read(&mut e, "mediaDocumentById", "bob", args.clone(), 1000).is_null());
    assert!(e.asset_access("bob", &asset.id, 1000).is_err());
    let secret2 = "fedcba9876543210fedcba9876543210";
    write(
        &mut e,
        "mediaLink",
        "service:ops",
        json!({"document":d["id"],"secret":secret2,"expiresAt":2000}),
        1000,
    );
    write(
        &mut e,
        "claimMedia",
        "carol",
        json!({"secret":secret2}),
        1000,
    );
    assert!(!read(&mut e, "mediaDocumentById", "carol", args.clone(), 1999).is_null());
    assert!(read(&mut e, "mediaDocumentById", "carol", args.clone(), 2000).is_null());
    assert!(e.asset_access("carol", &asset.id, 2000).is_err());
    assert!(!read(&mut e, "mediaDocumentById", "alice", args, 2000).is_null());
}

#[test]
fn public_metadata_self_join_does_not_publish_private_payload() {
    let mut e = engine("table clubCatalogue:");
    for open in [false, true] {
        let c = write(
            &mut e,
            "catalogueClub",
            "alice",
            json!({"title":"visible","open":open,"body":"private"}),
            1000,
        );
        let args = json!({"club":c["id"]});
        assert_eq!(
            read(&mut e, "catalogueClubById", "bob", args.clone(), 1000)["title"],
            "visible"
        );
        assert!(read(&mut e, "clubSheet", "bob", args.clone(), 1000).is_null());
        if open {
            write(&mut e, "selfJoinClub", "bob", args.clone(), 1000);
            assert_eq!(
                read(&mut e, "clubSheet", "bob", args, 1000)["body"],
                "private"
            );
        } else {
            fail(&mut e, "selfJoinClub", "bob", args, 1000, "E_RULE");
        }
    }
}
#[test]
fn hidden_only_page_is_complete_empty_and_never_a_denial() {
    let source = "table readers:\n  scope: text <=20\n  principal: principal\n  unique scope, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable secrets:\n  scope: text <=20\n  body: text <=80\n  read <- exists readers[.scope, viewer]\n  online only\nquery page(c: cursor ?):\n  return secrets where .scope = 'private' first 20 by .id after c\n";
    let b = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
    seed(
        &mut e,
        "secrets",
        json!({"id":"s","scope":"private","body":"hidden"}),
    );
    let c = ctx(&e, "page", "bob", json!({"c":null}), 1000);
    let page = e.query("page", c).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(wire::to_json(&page.data), json!([]));
    assert!(page.complete);
    assert!(page.next.is_none());
    assert!(page.cut.is_none());
}

A rule cannot scan "all participants consented". Store one grant after a mutation checks uniquely addressed consent rows, and delete it when consent is withdrawn. This two-person recipe makes those exact probes readable to the organizer; it deliberately reveals consent to that organizer. For an arbitrary population use an authorized maintained count and compare it with the expected population (`guide recipes`, booking); never scan partly readable consent rows.

```quarry
table agreements:
  owner: principal
  first: principal
  second: principal
  immutable owner, first, second
  read <- .owner = viewer or .first = viewer or .second = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  online only
table consents:
  agreement: agreements
  principal: principal
  unique agreement, principal
  immutable agreement, principal
  read <- .principal = viewer or exists agreements[.agreement, owner = viewer]
  insert <- .principal = viewer and (exists agreements[.agreement, first = viewer] or exists agreements[.agreement, second = viewer])
  delete <- .principal = viewer
  online only
table access:
  agreement: agreements
  principal: principal
  unique agreement, principal
  read <- .principal = viewer or exists agreements[.agreement, first = viewer] or exists agreements[.agreement, second = viewer]
  insert <- exists agreements[.agreement, owner = viewer]
  delete <- exists agreements[.agreement, first = viewer] or exists agreements[.agreement, second = viewer]
  online only
mutation consent(agreement: agreements):
  return insert consents { agreement, principal: viewer }
mutation open(agreement: agreements):
  a = agreements[agreement] ! else NOT_FOUND
  require a.owner = viewer else NOT_OWNER
  require exists consents[agreement, a.first] and exists consents[agreement, a.second] else CONSENT_NEEDED
  return insert access { agreement, principal: a.owner }
mutation withdraw(agreement: agreements):
  delete consents[agreement, viewer]
  a = agreements[agreement] ! else NOT_FOUND
  g = access[agreement, a.owner]
  if g != null:
    delete access[g.id]
query granted(agreement: agreements):
  return exists access[agreement, viewer]
```

Participants can read and delete the grant, so withdrawal atomically removes it. Every consent or participant change must maintain this grant in the same transaction; avoid separate grant repair jobs that leave a disclosure window.

## Copy on reveal: an event opens the payload

Keep the secret in a private table. An authorized event mutation copies it into an independently readable table in the same commit. Returning a shape that omits a secret is not a column read rule. The private lookup already requires the owner; an outsider gets NOT_FOUND. Reveal is durable; undoing the event flag does not retract an already copied payload. To retract it, delete the copy.

```quarry
table drafts:
  owner: principal
  body: text 1..200
  immutable owner
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  online only
table revealed:
  draft: drafts
  body: text 1..200
  unique draft
  public 'the author explicitly published this copy'
  insert <- exists drafts[.draft, owner = viewer]
  online only
mutation draft(body: text 1..200):
  return insert drafts { owner: viewer, body }
mutation reveal(draft: drafts):
  d = drafts[draft] ! else NOT_FOUND
  return insert revealed { draft, body: d.body }
query publication(publication: revealed):
  return revealed[publication]
```

For time instead of an event, use `not before .opensAt` and `expire at .endsAt` (the timed-disclosure recipe above); clocks stay out of read rules.

## Share links: token enrollment, then principal reads

The supported link grants an authenticated visitor a principal row. Possession of a high-entropy token authorizes enrollment; the resulting read rule uses the principal. Token rows are program-readable, but expose no listing operation or token row result. Generate secrets outside Quarry and provision them with a service seat. Hashing, leakage prevention and delivery of the link belong to the app. Revoking the token prevents new enrollment; revoke grant rows separately to remove existing readers. A query argument alone is not a capability grant.

//! The catalog: every table (declared or generated for a maintain) with its
//! columns and indexes, plus the named shapes and functions, and where each
//! schema path was declared so a core validation issue can point at a line.

use crate::ast::*;
use crate::diag::{Diagnostic, Site, name_rewrite, unknown};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct TableInfo {
    pub through: Option<snapback4_core::schema::through::Through>,
    pub captures: BTreeMap<String, ScalarType>,
    pub recorded: BTreeMap<String, ScalarType>,
    pub decl: TableDecl,
    pub columns: BTreeMap<String, ScalarType>,
    pub indexes: Vec<IndexDecl>,
    /// Set when the table is a maintain's generated target.
    pub maintained: Option<MaintainDecl>,
}

impl TableInfo {
    pub fn total_unique(&self, index: &IndexDecl) -> bool {
        index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns.iter().all(|column| column == "id" || self.columns.get(column).is_some_and(|ty| !ty.optional))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.1 — a scan cursor carries declared components only;
    // uniqueness with a nullable component cannot resolve ties between nulls.
    pub fn total_cursor(&self, index: &IndexDecl) -> bool {
        index.columns.iter().any(|column| column == "id") || index.unique && index.words.is_none()
            && index.columns.iter().all(|column| match index.computed.get(column) {
                Some(expr) => super::time_indexes::key(&self.decl.name, &self.columns, column, expr).is_ok(),
                None => self.columns.get(column).is_some_and(|ty| !ty.optional),
            })
    }
}

//! The refusals card. Quarry examples are compiled by tests/guide.rs.
pub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Refusals

A refusal explains why an operation could not complete and how to correct it.
Application checks can supply their own codes for the client to handle.

```quarry
query checkedName(name: text <=80):
  require name != '' else NAME_REQUIRED
  return name
```

Every refusal is `{ code, family, message, detail?, site?, rewrite?, guide?, rule?,
retryable? }`: `code` and `family` are stable, `rewrite` compiles, `guide`
names a card, `rule` is the program's own code on a `require` or named unique refusal, and
`retryable` says the same request may succeed later. The engine's families:
input, schema, auth, rule (your `require` codes), constraint, bound, op,
asset, action, route, prediction, generation, store; HTTP admission adds limit.
The client adds link (a transport or HTTP response failure) and client (the client itself refused, as after `close()`).
Reads never raise a rule refusal: a
hidden row is null, and a page omits it.

## Client-visible refusal ladder

For an operation written as decode → asserted lookup → write → require:

| Rung | Client-visible answer |
| --- | --- |
| Argument decoding | `E_INPUT`; no statement ran |
| Hidden or missing `t[id] ! else NOT_FOUND` | `NOT_FOUND`; a nullable `t[id]` is null and does not refuse |
| Table write rule | `E_RULE` for a readable denied target; `NOT_FOUND` for a hidden update/delete target; hidden insert collisions stay `E_RULE` |
| Program `require` | The authored `else CODE`, if execution reaches this statement |

After decoding, authored statement order decides precedence: a require placed
before the write can win, while a later require cannot replace an earlier
lookup or write refusal. For enrolment without a hidden-parent lookup, see
`guide rule-recipes --section "Enrolment into hidden things"`. Transport guest
sessions and production authentication: SURF owns `guide personas`/`guide wire`.

## Which refusal fires first

An operation stops at the first refusal it reaches, and a mutation's writes
roll back with it. In order (each step verified against the engine):

1. **Arguments.** Every argument decodes against its declared type and bounds

pub fn bind_rule(catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, rule: &RuleDecl) -> Fallible<RuleExpr> {
    let bindings = RuleBindings { old: rule.bindings.first().cloned(), next: rule.bindings.get(1).cloned(), update: rule.effect == Effect::Update, insert: rule.effect == Effect::Insert, hops: rule.effect != Effect::Read, present: Vec::new() };
    rule_expr(catalog, info, &rule.value, &bindings).map_err(|error| {
        let mut error = super::first_paste::inherited_rule(catalog, info, rule).unwrap_or(error);
        if error.code == "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR" && rule.effect == Effect::Update && rule.bindings.is_empty()
            && error.rewrite == "write this as a named transition" {
            let source = format!("update (old, next) <- {}", crate::print::expression(&rule.value));
            error.rewrite = format!("name both row images: `{source}`");
            error.source_rewrite = Some(source);
            error.source_rewrite_kind = Some(crate::diag::SourceRewriteKind::NamedUpdate);
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.036#13.6 — the named form of a whole unnamed update rule, so the rewrite keeps every other clause.
        if error.code == "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR" && rule.effect == Effect::Update && rule.bindings.is_empty() && error.rewrite.starts_with("name both images") {
            let comparison = error.message.split('`').nth(1).unwrap_or_default().to_owned();
            error.rewrite = super::rule_operator::named_update(&info.decl.name, &rule.value, &comparison);
        }
        if error.code == "E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP" {
            error.rewrite = match rule.effect {
                Effect::Read => reference::read_hop_rewrite(catalog, info, &rule.value).unwrap_or_else(|| "a read rule probes one hop: copy the deciding value onto this row (immutable, checked against the parent by the insert rule), or use read through .parent".into()),
                _ => reference::reference_rewrite(catalog, info, &rule.value),
fn rule_expr(catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, expr: &Expr, bindings: &RuleBindings) -> Fallible<RuleExpr> {
    let site = expr.site();
    match expr {
        Expr::Name { name, .. } => match name.as_str() {
            "allow" => Ok(RuleExpr::Allow),
            "deny" => Ok(RuleExpr::Deny),
            "public" => Ok(RuleExpr::Public { reason: format!("{} is public", info.decl.name) }),
            "true" => Ok(RuleExpr::Allow),
            "false" => Ok(RuleExpr::Deny),
            other => Err(unknown(site, format!("`{other}` is not a rule; rules are allow, deny, public, exists, created, and comparisons over ., viewer and literals"), "read <- .ownerId = viewer")),
        },
        Expr::Call { name, args, .. } if name == "public" => {
            let reason = match args.first() {
                Some(Expr::Literal { value: crate::ast::Literal::Str(reason), .. }) => reason.clone(),
                _ => format!("{} is public", info.decl.name),
            };
            Ok(RuleExpr::Public { reason })
        }
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } => match op.as_str() {
            "and" | "or" => {
                let left = rule_expr(catalog, info, left, bindings)?;
                let mut guarded = bindings.clone();
                guarded.present.extend(present_terms(&left, op == "and"));
                let right = rule_expr(catalog, info, right, &guarded)?;
                Ok(if op == "and" { RuleExpr::And(vec![left, right]) } else { RuleExpr::Or(vec![left, right]) })
            }
            "=" | "!=" | ">=" | ">" | "<=" | "<" => {
                let ordered = !matches!(op.as_str(), "=" | "!=");
                let transition = bindings.update && bindings.old.is_some();
                let effect = super::rule_operator::effect_word(bindings.insert, bindings.update, !bindings.hops);
                // @ref LLP 3000.036#11 — unsupported terms and misplaced ordered comparisons name what rules evaluate and move the author's comparison into the mutation.
                if let Some(what) = [left.as_ref(), right.as_ref()].into_iter().find_map(super::rule_operator::unsupported_side) {
                    return Err(super::rule_operator::diagnostic(site, &info.decl.name, effect, transition, expr, what));
                }
                if ordered && !transition && !bindings.insert { return Err(super::rule_operator::diagnostic(site, &info.decl.name, effect, transition, expr, "an ordered comparison")); }
                let (mut l, mut lt) = rule_term(catalog, info, left, bindings)?;
                let (mut r, mut rt) = rule_term(catalog, info, right, bindings)?;
                // `viewer = 'service:ranker'`: a text literal beside the
                // viewer names a principal, as it does beside `=` in a program.
                let as_principal = |term: &mut RuleTerm, ty: &mut Ty| {
fn rule_term(catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, expr: &Expr, bindings: &RuleBindings) -> Fallible<(RuleTerm, Ty)> {
    let site = expr.site();
    match expr {
        Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer" => Ok((RuleTerm::Viewer, Ty::Principal)),
        Expr::Member { base, name, .. } => match base.as_ref() {
            Expr::Read(read) if read.keys.len() == 1 && read.keys[0].column.as_deref().is_none_or(|c| c == "id")
                && read.range.is_none() && read.filter.is_none() && read.matching.is_none()
                && read.index.is_none() && read.seeks.is_empty() => {
                let result = rule_reference(catalog, info, &read.keys[0].value, name, bindings, site)?;
                if result.0.references().last().is_some_and(|(table, _)| table == read.table) { Ok(result) }
                else { Err(type_error(site, "the lookup table differs from the reference type", "use the reference's declared table")) }
            }
            Expr::Focal { .. } => {
                if bindings.update && bindings.old.is_some() {
                    return Err(unknown(site, "an update (old, next) rule names its images; . is not bound", format!("next.{name}")));
                }
                let ty = column_ty(info, name).ok_or_else(|| unknown(site, format!("rule reads undeclared column {}.{name}", info.decl.name), name_rewrite(name, info.columns.keys().map(String::as_str).chain(["id"]), None)))?;
                Ok((RuleTerm::Field(name.clone()), ty))
            }
            Expr::Name { name: binder, .. } if bindings.old.as_deref() == Some(binder) => {
                let ty = column_ty(info, name).ok_or_else(|| unknown(site, format!("rule reads undeclared column {}.{name}", info.decl.name), name_rewrite(name, info.columns.keys().map(String::as_str).chain(["id"]), None)))?;
                Ok((RuleTerm::OldField(name.clone()), ty))
            }
            Expr::Name { name: binder, .. } if bindings.next.as_deref() == Some(binder) => {
                let ty = column_ty(info, name).ok_or_else(|| unknown(site, format!("rule reads undeclared column {}.{name}", info.decl.name), name_rewrite(name, info.columns.keys().map(String::as_str).chain(["id"]), None)))?;
                Ok((RuleTerm::NextField(name.clone()), ty))
            }
            Expr::Name { name: binder, .. } if bindings.update && bindings.old.is_none()
                && matches!(binder.as_str(), "old" | "next") => Err(Diagnostic::new(site,
                    "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR",
                    format!("`{binder}.{name}` names a row image, but this update rule did not bind old and next"),
                    "write this as a named transition").guide("rules")),
            other => rule_reference(catalog, info, other, name, bindings, site),
        },
        Expr::Name { name, .. } if bindings.update && (bindings.old.as_deref() == Some(name) || bindings.next.as_deref() == Some(name)) => Err(unknown(site, format!("select a field of {name} explicitly"), format!("{name}.id"))),
        Expr::Name { name, .. } => Err(unknown(site, format!("`{name}` is not a rule term; rules read the row as .{name}"), format!(".{name}"))),
        Expr::Literal { value, .. } => Ok(match value {
            Literal::Str(text) => (RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(text.clone())), Ty::Text(text.chars().count() as u32)),
            Literal::Number(text) => (RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Int(super::integer_literal(text, site)?)), Ty::Int),
            Literal::Bool(b) => (RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Bool(*b)), Ty::Bool),
            Literal::Null => (RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Null), Ty::Null),
        }),
        Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } if op == "-" => {
            let (term, _) = rule_term(catalog, info, value, bindings)?;
            match term {
                RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Int(n)) => Ok((RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Int(-n)), Ty::Int)),
                _ => Err(type_error(site, "a negative rule literal needs an integer", "compare .column with an integer literal")),
            }
        }
        other => {
            let _ = catalog;
            Err(unknown(site, format!("`{}` is not a rule term", crate::print::expression(other)), "compare .column with viewer or a literal"))
        }
    }
}

    notes.extend(completeness_notes(&catalog, &schema, &programs));

    fn maintenance_operand(&self, ty: &Ty, site: &Site) -> Fallible<()> {
        match ty {
            Ty::Row(table) | Ty::WriteRow(table) | Ty::Page(table, _) => {
                if self.schema.row_view_predicate_field(table, "recountPending").is_some() {
                    return Err(Diagnostic::new(site, "E_VIEW_KEY", format!("value contains {table}.recountPending/pending maintenance state"),
                        "project the field you need first (value, member identity or copied member fields); return maintenance state only as query data").guide("maintains"));
                }
            },
            Ty::Optional(item) | Ty::List(item, _) | Ty::Collection(item, _) => self.maintenance_operand(item, site)?,
            Ty::Object(fields) | Ty::RowShape(fields) | Ty::RowObject(fields) => for ty in fields.values() { self.maintenance_operand(ty, site)?; },
            _ => {},
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    pub(super) fn computation(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        let prior = std::mem::replace(&mut self.returned_data, false);
        let result = self.expr(expr, out);
        self.returned_data = prior;
        result
    }

    fn expr_inner(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        let site = expr.site();
        match expr {
            A::Name { name, .. } if self.binding(name).is_none() && self.catalog.view_reads.contains_key(name) => {
                let mut merge = self.catalog.view_reads[name].clone();
                merge.site = site.clone();
                self.planned_view(&merge, out, prefix::Position::Page)
            }
            A::Name { name, .. } if self.binding(name).is_none() && self.catalog.tables.contains_key(name) => {
                self.read(&ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), table: name.clone(), keys: Vec::new(), seeks: Vec::new(), filter: None, index: Some("byId".into()),
                    order_keys: vec!["id".into()], range: None, matching: None, site: site.clone() }, out)
            }
            A::Name { name, .. } => self.name(name, site),
            A::Focal { .. } => match &self.focal {
                Some((var, ty)) => Ok((ir::var(var), ty.clone())),
                None => Err(unknown(site, "focal . is valid only inside a row shape", "name the row: `row.field`")),
            },
            A::Literal { value, .. } => Ok(match value {
                Literal::Str(text) => (Expr::Literal(Value::String(text.clone())), Ty::Text(text.chars().count() as u32)),
                Literal::Number(text) => (Expr::Literal(Value::Int(super::super::integer_literal(text, site)?)), Ty::Int),
                Literal::Bool(b) => (Expr::Literal(Value::Bool(*b)), Ty::Bool),
                Literal::Null => (Expr::Literal(Value::Null), Ty::Null),
            }),
            A::Array { items, .. } => {
                let mut exprs = Vec::new();
                let mut item_ty: Option<Ty> = None;
                let args = self.strict_args(items, out)?;
                let origins: Vec<_> = args.iter().zip(items).map(|((_, ty), source)| (ty.clone(), source.clone())).collect();
                for (e, t) in args {
                    item_ty = Some(match item_ty {
                        Some(j) => j.join_item(&t).map_err(|error| self.join_error_many(error.at("[]"), &origins))?,
                        None => t,
                    });
                    exprs.push(e);
                }
                let max = exprs.len() as u32;
                Ok((Expr::Array(exprs), Ty::List(Box::new(item_ty.unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown)), max)))
            }
            A::Object { fields, .. } => self.object(fields, out),
            A::Unary { op, value, .. } => {
                let (e, t) = self.expr(value, out)?;
                if op == "not" {
                    if !t.is_bool() {
                        let operand = print::expression(value);
                        return Err(type_error(site, format!("not needs a bool, got {} — `not` binds tighter than a comparison, so `not {operand} = x` reads as `(not {operand}) = x`", t.name()),
                            format!("write `{operand} != x`, or parenthesize: `not ({operand} = x)`")));
                    }
                    Ok((Expr::Unary { op: UnaryOp::Not, value: Box::new(e) }, Ty::Bool))
                } else {
                    if !t.is_numeric() {
                        return Err(type_error(site, format!("unary - needs a present number, got {}", t.name()), "negate an int, decimal or money value"));
                    }
                    Ok((Expr::Unary { op: UnaryOp::Neg, value: Box::new(e) }, t))
                }
            }
            A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } => self.binary(op, left, right, site, out),
            A::If { then, condition, otherwise, .. } => self.conditional(then, condition, otherwise, out),
            A::Member { base, name, .. } => {
                // Generated maintain keys are literal path-named columns. Resolve
                // the full chain before ordinary member/reference traversal.
                if let Some((root, path)) = aggregate_path(expr) {
                    let (e, t) = self.computation(root, out)?;
                    if let Ty::Row(table) = t.required()
                        && let Some(info) = self.catalog.tables.get(&table)
                        && info.maintained.is_some() && info.columns.contains_key(&path)
                    {
                        let (value, ty) = self.member(e, t, &path, site)?;
                        return Ok((value, self.field_refinement(expr).unwrap_or(ty)));
                    }
                }
                let (e, t) = self.computation(base, out)?;
                if name == "length" && matches!(t.required(), Ty::Text(_) | Ty::List(..) | Ty::Page(..) | Ty::Collection(..)) {
                    return Err(unsupported(site, ".length", format!("count({})", print::expression(base))));
                }
                // @ref LLP 3000.036#12 — a required reference's field reads its row:
                // `x.ref.col` is `t[x.ref].col`, the same authorized point read
                // (null when the row is missing or hidden), bound once.
                // A recorded attribution column spelled `ref.col` names the value
                // copied at insert; the live read would be a second meaning.
                let recorded = matches!(base.as_ref(), A::Member { name: column, .. }
                    if self.catalog.tables.values().any(|info| info.recorded.contains_key(&format!("{column}.{name}"))));
                if let Ty::Ref(table) = &t && name != "id" && !recorded
                    && self.catalog.tables.get(table).is_some_and(|info| info.maintained.is_none() && info.decl.ephemeral.is_none())
                {
                    let local = self.temp("reference");
                    let runtime = self.declare(&local, t.clone());
                    out.push(Stmt::Const { name: runtime, value: e });
                    let key = ast::KeyPart { column: None, value: A::Name { name: local, site: site.clone() }, site: site.clone() };

    pub(super) fn expr_as_reference(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body, expected: &Ty) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        let (value, ty) = self.expr_as_identity(expr, out, expected)?;
        Ok(self.row_identity(value, ty, expected))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — contextual projection is exactly `.id`,
    // without a lookup; optional and cross-table rows still need an edit.
    pub(super) fn row_identity(&self, value: Expr, ty: Ty, expected: &Ty) -> (Expr, Ty) {
        if !ty.is_optional() && matches!(ty.required(), Ty::Row(_) | Ty::WriteRow(_)) {
            let table = match ty.required() { Ty::Row(t) | Ty::WriteRow(t) => t, _ => unreachable!() };
            if expected.required() == Ty::Ref(table.clone()) {
                return (ir::field(value, "id"), Ty::Ref(table));
            }
        }
        (value, ty)
    }

    /// Whether two values may meet in `=`, `!=` or a key position.
    pub fn comparable(&self, other: &Ty) -> bool {
        let a = self.required();
        let b = other.required();
        a == b || a.assignable_to(&b) || b.assignable_to(&a) || a == Ty::Null || b == Ty::Null
    }

    /// The type both branches can share. Row-kind conflicts are errors at
    /// their first join, including inside objects and collections.
    pub fn join(&self, other: &Ty) -> Result<Ty, JoinError> {
        self.join_inner(other, false)
    }

    /// Nonempty list items must never erase a conflict into Unknown: a later
    /// item could otherwise recover a type the earlier values do not have.
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.1 — literals and concatenation share sound item joins.
    pub(crate) fn join_item(&self, other: &Ty) -> Result<Ty, JoinError> {
        self.join_inner(other, true)
    }
